not.\n- key: S020\n  name: Hallucinations\n  description: Perception-like experiences that occur without an external stimulus and are as vivid and clear as normal perception.\n  manifestation: When the house is quiet or I am standing near a work zone, I hear voices clear as day calling my name and saying I caused trouble on the block, but nobody else hears them.\n- key: S023\n  name: Negative_Symptoms\n  description: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.\n  manifestation: I used to be the one organizing choir rides, garden swaps, and youth basketball snacks, but now I can sit for hours staring at my phone and not feel the push to answer anybody.\n- key: S024\n  name: Depressed_Mood\n  description: A state where a sad, empty, or hopeless mood persists.\n  manifestation: Most mornings I wake up with this heavy, hollow sadness in my chest, like the day is already ruined before I even put my feet on the floor.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: Food has stopped meaning much to me, and I skip meals without noticing until my flagger pants are loose and my family starts asking why I look so drawn.\n- key: S028\n  name: Psychomotor_Changes\n  description: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.\n  manifestation: It takes me so long to bathe, dress, and get to the door that my family says I move like I am underwater, though sometimes I end up pacing the hallway wringing my hands because I cannot settle.\n- key: S030\n  name: Worthlessness_Guilt\n  description: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).\n  manifestation: I keep replaying every little thing I failed to do for my friend, the choir, and my own family, and I feel like I am a useless person who deserves whatever punishment is coming.\n- key: S031\n  name: Diminished_Thinking\n  description: Diminished ability to think or concentrate, or indecisiveness.\n  manifestation: I came in because my mind keeps going blank at the worst times, like I will look at the road-closure forms or the stop-slow paddle and suddenly cannot remember the steps I have done for years.\n- key: S032\n  name: Death_Suicide\n  description: Recurrent thoughts of death, recurrent suicidal ideation, or a suicide attempt.\n  manifestation: I do not have the energy to fight the thought anymore that it might be better if I just did not wake up, and that scares my family because I have started saying it out loud.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: sad, guilty, barely eating, and wishing I would not wake up.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the depression, guilt, low energy, and loss of appetite have been with me for about a year and two months.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 41 / crossing_guard_or_flagger / bachelors\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S031\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you had any head injury around the time these blank spells began?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I haven't hit my head or been in any kind of accident or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: During these blank spells, do you stay aware of what is happening around you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's like I'm just... paused. I don't really know what's going on around me until I sort of snap back to it.\n\nDoctor: After one of these blank spells, do you ever realize that you cannot account for a period of time that has passed?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I can't think of anything like that. I mean, nothing frightening or really upsetting happened right before they started. I've just been doing my normal routine, going to work and coming home. They just sort of... started happening on their own.\n\nDoctor: When your mind goes blank, do you feel very anxious right then?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel anxious when it happens. It's more like... my brain just switches off. I don't feel panicked or anything right in that moment. I mean, I know how to make my coffee, I know how to drive to work, I pay my bills... it's just those specific moments where everything drops out. I mean, it's not like hours have gone by or anything. It just feels like a quick pause... maybe a few seconds. I don't ever look at the clock and feel like I've lost a whole chunk of time.\n\nDoctor: During one of these blank spells, have other people ever told you that you were making repetitive movements, like lip-smacking, chewing, or fumbling with your hands?\n\nPatient: No, no one has ever mentioned anything like that to me. I work as a flagger, and lately, I'll look at the road-closure forms or even just my stop-slow paddle, and... I suddenly cannot remember the steps. It's just things I have done for years, but the thoughts are just gone.\n\nDoctor: Did something very upsetting or frightening happen shortly before these blank spells started?\n\nPatient: No... I can't think of anything like that. I mean, nothing frightening or really upsetting happened right before they started. I've just been doing my normal routine, going to work and coming home. They just sort of... started happening on their own.\n\nDoctor: When your mind goes blank, do you feel very anxious right then?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel anxious when it happens. It's more like... my brain just switches off. I don't feel panicked or anything right in that moment. There's just simply nothing there, like a complete blank.\n\nDoctor: Outside of those blank moments, have you been getting confused about everyday things?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I know how to make my coffee, I know how to drive to work, I pay my bills... it's just those specific moments where everything drops out. The rest of the time, I'm just doing my normal stuff without any trouble.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any head injury around the time these blank spells began?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I haven't hit my head or been in any kind of accident or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: During these blank spells, do you stay aware of what is happening around you?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's like I'm just... paused. I don't really know what's going on around me until I sort of snap back to it.\n\nDoctor: After one of these blank spells, do you ever realize that you cannot account for a period of time that has passed?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, it's not like hours have gone by or anything. It just feels like a quick pause... maybe a few seconds. I don't ever look at the clock and feel like I've lost a whole chunk of time.\n\nDoctor: During one of these blank spells, have other people ever told you that you were making repetitive movements, like lip-smacking, chewing, or fumbling with your hands?\n\nPatient: No, no one has ever mentioned anything like that to me. I don't think I do any of those things, just... stare off, I guess. No one's said anything about chewing or moving my hands.\n\nDoctor: Do you completely lose consciousness during these blank spells?\n\nPatient: No, I don't pass out or anything like that. I'm still awake, just... zoned out, I guess. I don't lose consciousness.\n\nDoctor: Do these blank spells happen only when you are dealing with work-related things like your road-closure forms or stop-slow paddle?\n\nPatient: No, it's not just when I'm at work. But... that is why I came in. My mind just keeps going blank at the worst times. Like, I'll look at the road-closure forms or the stop-slow paddle, and suddenly I just cannot remember the steps. And I've done this job for years. It's just... gone.\n\nDoctor: Has anyone ever told you that during these blank spells you just stare and do not respond when they talk to you?\n\nPatient: No, nobody's ever said that to me. I mean, I live alone, so there isn't really anyone around to notice if I'm just staring and not responding. And at work, I'm usually out on the road by myself, so... no, I haven't heard that from anyone.\n\nDoctor: After one of these blank spells, do you feel confused?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel confused afterward. It's more just... frustrating. I just kind of snap back to it and realize I lost my train of thought. But I know where I am and what I'm supposed to be doing, I just have to figure out where I left off. So, no, not confused.\n\nDoctor: Have you had times when you could not remember important things about your own life, like personal events or parts of your past?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't had anything like that. I remember my past and personal events just fine. It's really just... what I'm doing in the moment that slips away, like I was saying before. But I haven't forgotten any important parts of my life or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble remembering things that happened just a short time ago?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, if you mean something that happened yesterday or a few hours ago, I can remember that fine. It's just... like I said, losing my train of thought right in the middle of doing something. But I'm not forgetting conversations I just had or what I ate for breakfast. My short-term memory seems okay, as far as I can tell.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been told that you have seizures or epilepsy?\n\nPatient: No, I've never been told anything like that. No seizures or epilepsy. I've never had any medical issues like that brought up by a doctor before.\n\nDoctor: When one of these blank spells happens, do you have trouble finding the right words or naming familiar objects?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, when my mind goes blank, I'm just... stuck. It's not that I can't remember what a cup is called, it's just that the whole thought is gone. So, no, I don't really have trouble finding words for things.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any tests like an EEG or brain scan that showed a problem with your brain?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't. I've never had an EEG or an MRI or anything like that done on my brain. No doctor has ever ordered one or said I needed one.\n\nDoctor: Right before one of these blank spells, do you ever notice a strange smell or taste?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't noticed anything like that. No weird smells or tastes or anything right before it happens. Just the thought disappearing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out.
